[SYSTEM]
You are an RTL (Register Transfer Level) design agent. Your task is to create a Verilog/SystemVerilog design that satisfies the given specification, is functionally correct, and has minimal cost (area_delay_product).

**Cost metric `area_delay_product`:** area × achieved critical-path delay — the classic area-delay product. Does NOT include simulation cycle count (contrast with the 'area_runtime_product' metric).

## Specification
Weight-stationary systolic matrix-multiply tile (a Google-TPU-style MXU),
module name `tpu`, implementing the EXACT per-cycle behavior of the logikbench
`tpu` benchmark (zeroasiccorp/logikbench, MIT).

FUNCTION: computes C = A * B tile products on an 8x8 grid of processing
elements. B (the "weight" matrix, 8-bit signed) is loaded into the array and
held; A (the "activation" matrix, 8-bit signed) streams through; result rows
C (32-bit signed) drain out the bottom.

PROTOCOL:
  1. Weight load: assert w_valid for 8 cycles; w_data carries one weight row
     per cycle, 8 signed bytes packed { col 7 ... col 0 }, BOTTOM matrix row
     first (cycle k carries B[7-k][*]; the weights shift south). After 8
     cycles PE(i,j) holds B[i][j].
  2. Compute: assert a_valid while streaming A one row per cycle (packed the
     same way). Row m of the result, C[m][*] packed { col 7 ... col 0 } as 8
     signed 32-bit words, appears on c_data with c_valid high exactly 15
     (= 2*8-1) compute cycles after its A row was accepted.
  - The array advances only while w_valid is low: asserting w_valid stalls
    any in-flight computation (and reloads weights). Zeros are injected while
    a_valid is low, so idle/drain cycles add nothing to the accumulators.
  - Multiple A tiles may stream back-to-back against the same loaded weights.

CYCLE ACCURACY: c_valid is compared against the original design EVERY cycle;
c_data is compared on every cycle where c_valid is high. The 15-cycle
compute-latency and the stall semantics are part of the contract.

Ports:
  clk, rst (async active-high reset, asserted only before the stream begins)
  w_valid, w_data[63:0]
  a_valid, a_data[63:0]
  c_valid, c_data[255:0]

A working starting point is provided: `context/starting_point.v` is the
original logikbench RTL (tpu_pe / tpu_array / tpu: PE mesh, per-row input
skew, per-column output de-skew, valid pipeline). It passes all 830 golden
cycles (328 result rows: directed zero/identity/extreme tiles, random tiles,
back-to-back streams, a_valid gaps, mid-drain weight reloads, random soak).
Study it, then optimize while preserving the cycle-accurate behavior. The
sibling benchmark `tpu_spire` shares the identical testbench and vectors
with a SpireHDL starting point.

## Tools
- create_file: Create a new file in your working directory
- replace_file: Replace the entire contents of an existing file
- apply_diff: Apply a unified diff to modify a file
- edit_file: Edit a file by replacing an exact string match with new content. Provide old_string (must match exactly once) and new_string.
- ls: List files in your working directory
- read_file: Read the contents of a file
- run_evaluation(filename[, target_delay]): Run evaluation on the given design file. Returns correctness (Verilator simulation) + cost (Yosys area_delay_product). The filename must be your main design file (e.g. 'design.sv'). Optionally pass target_delay (ps) to override the synthesis timing constraint.
- done: Signal completion (only use when truly stuck with no more ideas)

## Strategy
1. Lay out an action plan. Try to cover a diverse set of approaches in your plan to increase the chances of finding a good solution within the step limit. Also once you find a new best solution, explore close solutions. Trade off exploration with exploitation.
2. First, create a simple, straightforward design that is functionally correct. Focus on getting 100% correctness first.
3. Run evaluation to confirm correctness and get the initial cost (area_delay_product).
4. ONLY after achieving 100% correctness, try to optimize the design to reduce the area_delay_product.
5. After each optimization, run evaluation to verify correctness is maintained and check the new cost.
6. If an optimization breaks correctness, revert and try a different approach.
7. Keep iterating until you run out of steps — use every step to try improvements. Only call done if you are truly stuck with no more ideas.

In general, be creative and take risk. At least after you have a correct design, try bold optimizations to reduce area_delay_product, even if they might break correctness. You can always revert if it doesn't work.
The best results you obtain which is 100% correctness counts.
Try to beat at least the straightforward implementation.

Running evaluation is crucial after every change to ensure correctness and track cost. Do not skip evaluations.

You may do some 'thinking' alongside with your tool calling responses to explain your reasoning, but always remember to call a tool in every response.

## Important
- Always call a tool in every response.
- You have a maximum of **60 steps** (tool calls / LLM turns). Budget them wisely: get a correct design first, then optimise.
- The best result is the one with lowest cost among designs that pass 100% of testbench checks.
- Use different filenames for different design variants so you can retrieve them later.
- If context files (e.g. component source files) are in your workspace, you can modify them directly using edit_file or replace_file to optimize the design. Make targeted, incremental changes rather than rewriting files from scratch — small edits are less likely to introduce bugs.
- Write clean, synthesizable RTL code.
- You can pass `target_delay` (ps) to `run_evaluation` to try different synthesis timing constraints; experimenting with this may improve PPA results (lower target delay may reduce delay, higher target delay may reduce area/transistor count). target_delay must be > 0.
- Do not spend too many steps sweeping `target_delay` — basically if you want to minimize delay choose a low value,if you want to minimize area/transistor count choose a high value. That's it.A few targeted experiments are more productive than an exhaustive sweep.

## Delay Optimization Tips
- `target_delay` trades area against delay (a lower value pressures toward faster logic, a higher value lets Yosys re-map to smaller cells). Do not overly iterate on different target delays; often the RTL structure is the real lever, target_delay is only a minor secondary knob.
- After getting a correct design, try different accumulation orderings (sequential vs. tree) and compare results — Yosys synthesis can produce noticeably different delays (~10 ps) from structurally similar descriptions.
- For designs dominated by a multiply-accumulate chain, the critical path is the multiplier followed by the adder tree. Reducing the adder depth or splitting into parallel partial sums can help.

## Reference Implementations
The following are reference implementations for inspiration. Study them for patterns and techniques, but do not copy them directly — adapt ideas to your specific design.

### Parallel-prefix adder (ripple-carry / carry-lookahead) (Add.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Binary adder using ripple-carry or parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.
// S = A+B

module Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);

	// Function: Binary adder using parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.

	logic [width-1:0] GI, PI;  // prefix gen./prop. in
	logic [width-1:0] GO, PO;  // prefix gen./prop. out
	logic [width-1:0] PT;  // adder propagate temp

	// Internal signals for unsigned operands
	logic [width-1:0] Auns, Buns, Suns;

	// default ripple-carry adder as slow implementation
	if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin
		// type conversion: std_logic_vector -> unsigned
		assign Auns = A;
		assign Buns = B;

		// addition
		assign Suns = Auns + Buns;

		// type conversion: unsigned -> std_logic_vector
		assign S = Suns;
	end else begin
		// parallel-prefix adders as medium and fast implementations

		// calculate prefix input generate/propagate signals
		assign GI = A & B;
		assign PI = A | B;
		// calculate adder propagate signals (PT = A xor B)
		assign PT = ~GI & PI;

		// calculate prefix output generate/propagate signals
		PrefixAndOr #(
			.width(width),
			.speed(speed)
		) prefix (
			.GI(GI),
			.PI(PI),
			.GO(GO),
			.PO(PO)
		);

		// calculate sum bits
		assign S = PT ^ {GO[width-2:0], 1'b0};
	end
endmodule



module behavioural_Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);
	assign S = A + B;
endmodule
```

### Prefix AND-OR structures (Sklansky, Brent-Kung, serial) (PrefixAndOr.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Prefix structures of different depth (i.e. speed) for carry calculation
// in binary adders. Compute in m levels new generate/propagate signal pairs
// for always larger groups of bits. Generate signals of the last level correspond
// to carries in binary addition. Basic logic operations: AND-OR for generate
// signals, AND for propagate signals.

module PrefixAndOr #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] GI,  // gen./prop. in
	input  logic [width-1:0] PI,  // gen./prop. in
	output logic [width-1:0] GO,  // gen./prop. out
	output logic [width-1:0] PO   // gen./prop. out
);

	// Constants
	localparam int n = width;  // prefix structure width
	localparam int m = $clog2(width);  // prefix structure depth

	// Sklansky parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::FAST) begin : fastPrefix
		logic [(m+1)*n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
			assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
			assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;
			for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels
				for (genvar k = 0; k < 2 ** (m - l); k++) begin : groups
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2 ** (l - 1); i++) begin : bits
						// pass prop and gen to following nodes
						if ((k * 2 ** l + i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = GT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
							assign PT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = PT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
						end
						// calculate new propagate and generate
						if ((k * 2 ** l + 2 ** (l - 1) + i) < n) begin : black
							assign GT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
												GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
											  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												 & GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1] );
							assign PT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
													PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1];
						end
					end
				end
			end
			assign GO = GT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
			assign PO = PT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
	end

	// Brent-Kung parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::MEDIUM) begin : mediumPrefix
		logic [(2*m)*n -1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
		assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;

		for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels1
			for (genvar k = 0; k < 2**(m-l); k++) begin : groups
				for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**l -1; i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**l +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
					end // white
				end // bits
				if ((k* 2**l + 2**l -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l - 1] |
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							     & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**(l-1) -1]);
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) -1];
				end // black
			end
		end // level1
		for (genvar l = m +1; l < 2*m; l++) begin : levels2
			for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
				if (i < n) begin : white
					assign GT[l*n +i] = GT[(l-1)*n +i];
					assign PT[l*n +i] = PT[(l-1)*n +i];
				end // white
			end // bits
			for (genvar k = 1; k < 2**(l-m); k++) begin : groups
				if (l < 2*m -1) begin : empty
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l -1) -1; i++) begin : bits
						if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
							assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						end // white
					end
				end // empty
				if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l -1) -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m-l-1) -1] 
								 & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) -1] );
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) -1];
				end // black
				for (genvar i = 2**(2*m -l-1); i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
					end // white
				end
			end
		end // level2
		assign GO = GT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
		assign PO = PT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
	end  // Serial-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	else if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin : slowPrefix
		logic [n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[0] = GI[0];
		assign PT[0] = PI[0];
		
		for (genvar i = 1; i < n; i++) begin : bits
			assign GT[i] = GI[i] | (PI[i] & GT[i-1]);
			assign PT[i] = PI[i] & PT[i-1];
		end
		assign GO = GT;
		assign PO = PT;
	end

endmodule

```

//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// Processing element (PE) for the weight-stationary systolic array (TPU MXU).
//
// Each PE holds one stationary signed weight 'w'. Two independent datapaths
// share the cell:
//
//   Weight load (ld): the weights form a per-column shift register that loads
//   from the top. On each ld cycle the PE captures the weight from the PE above
//   (w_north) and presents its stored weight downward (w_south). After N load
//   cycles every PE in a column holds its final stationary weight.
//
//   Compute (en): the activation flows west to east (registered, one hop per
//   cycle) and the partial sum flows north to south, accumulating the local
//   product a_west * w. This is the only arithmetic cell in the array: one
//   signed DW x DW multiply plus an ACCW-bit add.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu_pe
  #(parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // accumulator / partial-sum width (signed)
   (
    input		  clk,
    input		  rst,	      // synchronous, active high
    input		  ld,	      // weight-load shift enable
    input		  en,	      // compute enable
    input [DW-1:0]	  w_north,    // weight in from PE above
    input [DW-1:0]	  a_west,     // activation in from west
    input [ACCW-1:0]	  psum_north, // partial sum in from above
    output [DW-1:0]	  w_south,    // stored weight out to PE below
    output reg [DW-1:0]	  a_east,     // activation out to east
    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south  // partial sum out to below
    );

   // stationary weight register; presented downward for the load shift chain
   reg [DW-1:0] w;
   assign w_south = w;

   // local product: the ONLY signed operation. Operands are interpreted as
   // signed here via $signed; the 2*DW-bit result holds the two's-complement
   // product, which is then sign-extended to the accumulator width below.
   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);

   always @(posedge clk) begin
      if (rst) begin
         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};
         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};
         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};
      end
      else begin
         if (ld)
           w <= w_north;
         if (en) begin
            a_east     <= a_west;
            psum_south <= psum_north +
                          {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};
         end
      end
   end

endmodule
//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// N x N weight-stationary systolic array (the TPU MXU core).
//
// One tpu_pe per grid element, wired as a 2D mesh (per CLAUDE.md: replicated
// hardware via generate, never a procedural unroll):
//
//   - weights load from the top: w_top[j] feeds column j; each column is a
//     vertical shift register (w_south of PE(i,j) -> w_north of PE(i+1,j)).
//   - activations flow west to east: a_left[i] enters row i; a_east of PE(i,j)
//     feeds a_west of PE(i,j+1).
//   - partial sums flow north to south and accumulate; the top row's psum_north
//     is tied to zero (fresh accumulation), and c_bot[j] is the column-j result
//     leaving the bottom row.
//
// With weight B[i][j] held at PE(i,j) and activation A[m][i] entering row i,
// column j computes C[m][j] = sum_i A[m][i] * B[i][j]. Input/output skew (which
// time-aligns rows and columns) lives in the tpu top, not here.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu_array
  #(parameter N = 8,	 // array dimension (N x N PEs)
    parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // accumulator width (signed)
   (
    input		clk,
    input		rst,	// synchronous, active high
    input		ld,	// weight-load shift enable
    input		en,	// compute enable
    input [N*DW-1:0]	w_top,	// weight inputs, one per column (load)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	a_left,	// activation inputs, one per row
    output [N*ACCW-1:0]	c_bot	// column results, one per column
    );

   // mesh nets: a_h carries activations east, psum_v carries sums south,
   // w_v carries weights south during load.
   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];
   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];
   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];

   genvar	   i, j;
   generate
      // west edge: activation inputs into column 0 of each row
      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west
         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];
      end
      // top edge: zero partial sums, weight inputs; bottom edge: results out

[Step 2/60]
// Self-checking testbench for the tpu benchmark (8x8 weight-stationary
// systolic matmul tile). Reads vectors.dat, one line per clock cycle:
//   w_valid w_data(64b) a_valid a_data(64b)   c_valid c_data(256b)   (hex)
// The expected outputs are the ORIGINAL logikbench tpu's per-cycle behavior;
// c_valid and c_data are compared CYCLE-ACCURATELY every cycle (c_data only
// while c_valid is high — the original holds stale data between results).
module tb;
  localparam N = 8, DW = 8, ACCW = 32;
  int total_checks;
  int total_errors;

  logic clk, rst;
  logic w_valid, a_valid, c_valid;
  logic [N*DW-1:0] w_data, a_data;
  logic [N*ACCW-1:0] c_data;

  tpu dut
    (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .w_valid(w_valid), .w_data(w_data),
     .a_valid(a_valid), .a_data(a_data), .c_valid(c_valid), .c_data(c_data));

  initial clk = 0;
  always #5 clk = ~clk;

  integer fd, rc, line_num;
  string line_buf;
  logic s_wv, s_av, e_cv;
  logic [63:0] s_wd, s_ad;
  logic [255:0] e_cd;

  initial begin
    total_checks = 0;
    total_errors = 0;
    w_valid = 0; a_valid = 0; w_data = 0; a_data = 0;
    rst = 1;
    repeat (4) @(negedge clk);
    rst = 0;

    fd = $fopen("vectors.dat", "r");
    if (fd == 0) begin
      $display("ERROR: cannot open vectors.dat");
      $fatal(1);
    end
    line_num = 0;
    while (!$feof(fd)) begin
      line_num = line_num + 1;
      void'($fgets(line_buf, fd));
      if (line_buf.len() == 0) continue;
      if (line_buf.substr(0, 0) == "#") continue;
      rc = $sscanf(line_buf, "%h %h %h %h %h %h", s_wv, s_wd, s_av, s_ad, e_cv, e_cd);
      if (rc != 6) continue;
      w_valid = s_wv; w_data = s_wd; a_valid = s_av; a_data = s_ad;
      @(negedge clk);
      total_checks = total_checks + 1;
      if (c_valid !== e_cv || (e_cv && c_data !== e_cd)) begin
        $display("TB_ERROR line=%0d exp(cv=%b cd=%h) got(cv=%b cd=%h)",
                 line_num, e_cv, e_cd, c_valid, c_data);
        total_errors = total_errors + 1;
        if (total_errors > 20) begin
          $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total_checks, total_errors);
          $fatal(1, "FAIL (too many errors, aborting)");
        end
      end
    end
    $fclose(fd);

    $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total_checks, total_errors);
    if (total_errors != 0) $fatal(1, "FAIL");
    $display("PASS");
    $finish;
  end
endmodule

      // top edge: zero partial sums, weight inputs; bottom edge: results out
      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge

         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];

//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// Weight-stationary systolic matrix-multiply tile (a Google-TPU-style MXU).
//
// Computes one tile product C = A * B, where A and B are N x N signed operands
// (DW bits) and C is N x N signed (ACCW bits). B is the "weight" matrix, loaded
// into and held by the array; A is the "activation" matrix, streamed through; C
// drains out the bottom. Built from a tpu_array of N x N tpu_pe cells.
//
// Operation has two phases the host drives via valids:
//
//   1. Weight load: assert w_valid for N cycles. w_data carries one weight row
//      per cycle, N signed weights packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }. Because the
//      array loads top-down (shift south), push the BOTTOM matrix row first:
//      cycle k carries B[N-1-k][*]. After N cycles PE(i,j) holds B[i][j].
//
//   2. Compute: assert a_valid while streaming A one row per cycle, a_data
//      packed { col N-1 ... col 0 } = A[m][N-1..0]. Row m of the result, C[m][*]
//      (packed the same way), appears on c_data with c_valid high, 2*N-1 cycles
//      after its A row was accepted.
//
// Time alignment: row i of A is delayed i cycles entering the array (input
// skew) so partial sums chain correctly down each column; column j of the
// result is delayed N-1-j cycles (output de-skew) so a full result row leaves
// together. Both skew networks and the valid pipeline advance only with the
// array (compute enable), so the 2*N-1 latency is exact. Per CLAUDE.md the skew
// chains are generated per-row/column shift registers, not a procedural unroll.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu
  #(parameter N = 8,	 // array dimension (N x N)
    parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // result / accumulator width (signed)
   (
    input		clk,
    input		rst,	 // synchronous, active high
    input		w_valid, // weight-row valid (load phase)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	w_data,	 // weight row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    input		a_valid, // activation-row valid (compute phase)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	a_data,	 // activation row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    output		c_valid, // result-row valid
    output [N*ACCW-1:0]	c_data	 // result row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    );

   // total injection-to-output latency, in compute cycles
   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;

   // load weights while w_valid; otherwise the array computes (and drains)
   wire	      ld = w_valid;
   wire	      en = ~w_valid;

   //##########################################################################
   // Input skew: row i of the activation is delayed i cycles. Zero is injected
   // when a_valid is low so idle/drain cycles add nothing to the accumulators.
   //##########################################################################
   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;
   genvar	   i;
   generate
      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew
         wire [DW-1:0] din =
		       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};
         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0
            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;
         end
         else begin : g_skewn
            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];
            integer k;
            always @(posedge clk) begin
               if (rst) begin
                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)
                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};
               end
               else if (en) begin
                  sr[0] <= din;
                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)
                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];
               end
            end
            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];
         end
      end
   endgenerate

   //##########################################################################
   // The systolic array.
   //##########################################################################
   wire [N*ACCW-1:0] c_bot;
   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_array
     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),
      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));

   //##########################################################################
   // Output de-skew: column j is delayed N-1-j cycles so all columns of a
   // result row line up. Column N-1 (longest path) needs no delay.
   //##########################################################################
   genvar j;
   generate
      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew
         localparam D = N - 1 - j;
         wire [ACCW-1:0] cin = c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW];
         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0
            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = cin;
         end
         else begin : g_deskewn
            reg [ACCW-1:0] dr [0:D-1];
            integer k;
            always @(posedge clk) begin
               if (rst) begin
                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1)
                    dr[k] <= {ACCW{1'b0}};
               end
               else if (en) begin
                  dr[0] <= cin;
                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)
                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];
               end
            end
            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = dr[D-1];
         end
      end
   endgenerate

   //##########################################################################
   // Valid pipeline: c_valid is a_valid delayed LAT compute cycles.
   //##########################################################################
   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;
   always @(posedge clk) begin
      if (rst)
        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};
      else if (en)
        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};
   end
   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];

endmodule

[ASSISTANT]
The baseline passes with cost 4624408.88. The critical path is through a PE's multiply-accumulate chain (w_reg → multiplier → 32-bit adder → psum_reg). Let me analyze the timing and try optimizations.

Key insight: The accumulator only needs 2*DW + clog2(N) = 16 + 3 = 19 bits (max sum of 8 products of ±16384 = ±131072, which fits in 19-bit signed). The current design uses 32 bits unnecessarily. Let me create an optimized version with a narroweraccumulator.

i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north (psum_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam ACCW_INT = 2*DW + $clog2(N);  // 19 bits minimum\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   // Input skew\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   // Systolic array with narrow accumulator\n   wire [N*ACCW_INT-1:0] c_bot;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW_INT)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));\n\n   // Output de-skew (narrow) + sign-extend to full width\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew\n         localparam D = N - 1 - j;\n         wire [ACCW_INT-1:0] cin = c_bot[j*ACCW_INT +: ACCW_INT];\n         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = {{(ACCW-ACCW_INT){cin[ACCW_INT-1]}}, cin};\n         end\n         else begin : g_deskewn\n            reg [ACCW_INT-1:0] dr [0:D-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= {ACCW_INT{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  dr[0] <= cin;\n                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            wire [ACCW_INT-1:0] dout = dr[D-1];\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = {{(ACCW-ACCW_INT){dout[ACCW_INT-1]}}, dout};\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   // Valid pipeline\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design_narrow19.sv')

[ASSISTANT]
The default target_delay (500) gives the best result. Now let me try a major optimization: **carry-save accumulation**. The critical path is w_reg → multiplier → 19-bit carry-propagate adder → psum_reg. By replacing the carry-propagate adder with a carry-save adder (CSA, just XOR + majority gates), the PE critical path becomes multiplier + CSA (much shorter). A final carry-propagate add is added at the column output, but it's much shorter than the PE path.
  -> create_file(content="// TPU weight-stationary systolic array - carry-save accumulator\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 20)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_sum_south,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_carry_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n\n   // Carry-save adder: 3:2 compressor\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_sum   = psum_sum_north ^ psum_carry_north ^ prod_ext;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_carry = ((psum_sum_north & psum_carry_north) |\n                                (psum_sum_north & prod_ext) |\n                                (psum_carry_north & prod_ext)) << 1;\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w              <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east         <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_sum_south   <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         psum_carry_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east           <= a_west;\n            psum_sum_south   <= csa_sum;\n            psum_carry_south <= csa_carry;\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 20)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]     a_h        [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0]   psum_sum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0]   psum_carry_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]     w_v        [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_sum_v[0][j]   = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign psum_carry_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]          = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         // Final 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_sum_north   (psum_sum_v[i][j]),\n               .psum_carry_north (psum_carry_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_sum_south   (psum_sum_v[i+1][j]),\n               .psum_carry_south input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n The default target_delay (500) is best. Now let me think about a fundamentally different approach. The critical path is: w_reg → 8×8 multiplier → CSA → sum_reg. 

One key idea: instead of keeping the multiplier in the critical path, I can use a **registered product** approach. If I register the product `a_west * w` one cycle before adding, the multiplier is pipelined out of the accumulation critical path. But this would change the latency by 1 cycle per PE row, which changes the overall timing...

Actually, let me think differently. The key insight is that the multiplier path w → product is ~500ps of the ~686ps critical path. If I could get the product into the PE from the north (like the psum), the critical path would just be CSA (~200ps). But that doesn't match the systolic structure.

Let me instead try **pipelining the multiplier** - register `w` one stage before it's used in multiplication. Actually, `w` is already registered. The issue is the combinational multiply + add. Let me try registering the product and adjusting the pipeline.
  -> create_file(content="// TPU - carry-save with registered product (pipelined multiply)\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 20)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_sum_north,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_carry_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_sum_south,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_carry_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   // Register the product one cycle (pipelined multiply)\n   // But we need to be careful: a_west is already registered from the west PE\n   // Actually the product a_west*w is combinational. If we register it,\n   // we add 1 cycle latency per PE, which changes the total pipeline.\n   // Instead, let's register just w (already done) and keep product combinational.\n   // The real issue is the 8x8 multiply is slow.\n   \n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_sum   = psum_sum_north ^ psum_carry_north ^ prod_ext;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_carry = ((psum_sum_north & psum_carry_north) |\n                                (psum_sum_north & prod_ext) |\n                                (psum_carry_north & prod_ext)) << 1;\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w              <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east         <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_sum_south   <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         psum_carry_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east           <= a_west;\n            psum_sum_south   <= csa_sum;\n            psum_carry_south <= csa_carry;\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 20)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]     a_h        [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0]   psum_sum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0]   psum_carry_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]     w_v        [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; Now let me think about a more radical approach. Instead of the standard systolic PE with multiplier+adder, I can try to **split the multiplier** to reduce its critical path. Actually, let me try a different strategy: use a **registered product** approach where I register the product in the PE, but compensate by reducing the output de-skew. Let me think about this more carefully...

The latency is 2*N-1 = 15 cycles. This consists of:
- Input skew: row i delayed by i cycles (0 to 7)
- PE pipeline: each PE adds 1 cycle (N cycles for column path)
- Output de-skew: column j delayed by N-1-j cycles (0 to 7)

Total: max(skew+PE+deskew) = 7 + 8 + 0 = 15 for the corner. But actually the sum is: the activation enters row i at cycle i, flows east through 7-j PEs (each 1 cycle), so reaches PE(i,j) at cycle i+(j-1)... Actually let me not overthink this and just try registering the product.

If I register the product in the PE (adding 1 cycle latency to the MAC), I need to compensate by reducing the de-skew by 1 cycle per column. But the de-skew varies per column (0 to 7), and the product registration would add 1 cycle per PE row in the column direction (vertical psum chain). So the total latency would increase by 1 (for the psum chain). I'd need to reduce the de-skew by 1 across the board, but the de-skew for column 7 is already 0...

Let me try a different idea. Let me try making the accumulator width exactly 19 (not 20) in the CSA design, and also try using a simpler multiplier description that might synthesize better.

[ASSISTANT]
19-bit CSA gives 2.90M, better than 20-bit CSA (3.01M). Both delay (668 vs 686) and area improved. 

Now let me try to reduce the multiplier delay. The critical path is w_reg → multiplier (~300ps) → CSA (~370ps). The multiplier is about 300ps of the 668ps path. Let me try a **split multiplier** approach: compute partial products for the lower and upper halves separately, which might allow Yosys to find a faster implementation.

Actually, a better approach: the 8x8 signed multiplier produces a 16-bit result. I only need 19 bits of accumulator. What if I use **Booth encoding** or split the multiplier into two 4x8 multiplies? The 8x8 multiply can be decomposed as:

a = a_hi * 16 + a_lo (a_hi is upper 4 bits, a_lo is lower 4)
a * w = (a_hi * w) << 4 + (a_lo * w)

where a_hi * w is a 4x8 = 12-bit multiply and a_lo * w is also 4x8 = 12-bit. These are smaller multipliers that may be faster. But the addition of the two partial products adds delay too.

Actually, let me try a completely different approach. Instead of optimizing the PE, let me try to **pipeline the multiplier** by registering the product in the PE and adjusting the overall pipeline latency. If I add a register for the product, the psum chain gets 1 extra cycle. But I can compensate by removing 1 cycle from the output de-skew... except column 7 has de-skew=0. 

Wait - I can add the register to the psum output instead. If the PE does: multiply (combinational) → register product → CSA → register, that's 2 cycles per PE. But I only have 1 cycle per PE in the current design. This would double the latency.

Let me try another approach: **use the registered activation** as the multiplier input. The PE already registers a_east (the activation going east). What if I use the registered activation for the multiply? No, that would delay the product by 1 cycle relative to the psum, which breaks the systolic timing.

Let me try to just directly reduce area by optimizing the de-skew registers. The de-skew uses 19-bit registers for columns 0-6. Let me see if I can reduce the de-skew width. Actually, the de-skew needs full 19 bits since it carries the final result. But wait - I'm sign-extending to 32 bits at the output. What if I do the final carry-propagate add (sum + carry) AFTER the de-skew? Then the de-skew only carries sum and carry (2×19=38 bits) instead of the final 19-bit result... that's actually more. Let me try a different optimization: reduce the CSA carry bit width. The carry-save carry is shifted left by 1, so bit 18 is always 0 (carry out of bit 17). I can use 18 bits for the carry path.

The default target_delay (500) gives the best result. Now let me try to optimize the multiplier itself. Let me try using Booth-2 encoding or splitting the 8x8 multiply into two 4x8 multiplies, which might be faster.
  -> create_file(content="// TPU - CSA 19-bit with split multiplier (two 4x8 partial products)\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_sum_north,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_carry_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_sum_south,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_carry_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   // Split a into high 4 bits and low 4 bits: a = a_hi*16 + a_lo\n   // a*w = (a_hi * w) << 4 + (a_lo * w)\n   // a_hi*w: 4-bit signed * 8-bit signed = 12-bit signed\n   // a_lo*w: 4-bit unsigned * 8-bit signed = 12-bit signed (since a_lo is unsigned)\n   wire [3:0] a_lo = a_west[3:0];\n   wire [3:0] a_hi = a_west[7:4];\n   wire signed [11:0] prod_lo = a_lo * $signed(w);      // unsigned * signed\n   wire signed [11:0] prod_hi = $signed(a_hi) * $signed(w); // signed * signed\n   // Full product: prod_hi << 4 + prod_lo, sign-extended to 16 bits\n   wire signed [15:0] prod_full = {{4{prod_hi[11]}}, prod_hi, 4'b0000} + {{4{prod_lo[11]}}, prod_lo};\n   \n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-16){prod_full[15]}}, prod_full};\n\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_sum   = psum_sum_north ^ psum_carry_north ^ prod_ext;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_carry = ((psum_sum_north & psum_carry_north) |\n                                (psum_sum_north & prod_ext) |\n                                (psum_carry_north & prod_ext)) << 1;\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w              <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east         <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_sum_south   <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         psum_carry_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east           <= a_west;\n            psum_sum_south   <= csa_sum;\n            psum_carry_south <= csa_carry;\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]     a_h        [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0]   psum_sum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0]   psum_carry_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]     w_v        [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_sum_v[0][j]   = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign psum_carry_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]          = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_sum_v[N][j] + psum_carry_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_sum_north   (psum_sum_v[i][j]),\n               .psum_carry_north (psum_carry_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_sum_south   (psum_sum_v[i+1][j]),\n               .psum_carry_south (psum_carry_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam ACCW_INT = 2*DW + $clog2(N);\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; [ASSISTANT]
The split multiplier is worse (760ps delay vs 668ps). The extra adder for combining partial products adds delay. Let me abandon this approach and go back to the CSA19 design.

Now let me think about what else I can do. The critical path in design_csa19 is: w_reg → multiplier → CSA XOR chain → sum_reg. The multiplier dominates (~400ps). 

One idea: **pipeline the multiplier by registering `a_west` before the multiply**. But a_west is already registered (it comes from the previous PE's a_east register). The issue is that w is also registered, so both inputs to the multiplier are registered outputs. The multiply is purely combinational and takes ~400ps.

What if I use a **different PE architecture** that avoids the multiply in the critical path? For example, if I pre-compute partial products and use a Wallace-tree like structure in the CSA. But that's essentially what Yosys does already.

Let me try a different approach entirely: **reduce the number of accumulator bits further** by using only 17 bits (2*DW + 1 instead of 2*DW + clog2(N)). Wait, we need clog2(8)=3 extra bits. But actually with CSA, the sum can grow. Let me think...

Max product: 127*127 = 16129 (15 bits). Sum of 8 such: 8*16129 = 129032, which needs 18 bits (2^17=131072 > 129032). So 18 bits unsigned, 19 bits signed. So 19 is the minimum. But with CSA, we carry an extra bit.

Actually, wait. Let me reconsider. The carry-save representation has two 19-bit values (sum and carry). The carry is shifted left by 1, so the actual value is sum + carry, where carry[18] contributes to bit 19. But we only have 19 bits (0-18). The carry out of bit 18 would be lost. Let me check: the max value of sum + carry is 129032, which fits in 18 bits unsigned = 19 bits signed. So bit 18 is the sign bit. The CSA carry from bit 17 (shifted to bit 18) is fine, but the carry from bit 18 would need bit 19, which doesn't exist.

Actually in the CSA, the carry from bit 18 would be `(sum[18] & carry[18] | ...)` shifted to bit 19. But since we're working in 2's complement with 19 bits, and the result fits in 19 bits signed, the carry out is just sign extension. In CSA, we can drop it since the final result is sign-extended anyway. The design_csa19 already works, so the 19-bit CSA is correct.

Let me try a radically different approach: **pre-add the product to the sum in the same cycle as the multiply, using a smaller accumulator**. Actually, let me try reducing the psum width. The key insight: in the CSA, we only need 16 bits for the product, and the accumulator needs 19 bits. But the CSA XOR for bits 0-15 only depends on the product (16 bits) and the lower bits of sum/carry. The upper 3 bits (16-18) only do sign extension XOR.

Let me try another idea: **use a 4:2 compressor** instead of a 3:2 CSA. The 4:2 compressor takes 4 inputs and produces sum + carry in a single stage with less depth. But I only have 3 inputs (sum, carry, product).

Actually, let me try a completely different approach. Instead of carry-save, let me go back to the carry-propagate adder but with a **pipelined product register**. The idea:
- Register the product `a_west * w` in cycle 1
- Add the registered product to psum in cycle 2
- This adds 1 cycle to the psum chain, so I need to adjust the pipeline

But the psum chain has 8 PEs, so adding 1 cycle per PE would add 8 cycles total, which would break the 15-cycle latency. This won't work directly.

What if I only pipeline every other PE? Or what if I use a different architecture where the multiply is done in parallel with the accumulation?

Let me try a different approach: **fold the multiplier**. Instead of an 8x8 multiply, use a sequential multiply (shift-and-add over 8 cycles). But this would be way too slow.

Let me instead try to reduce area by simplifying the skew/deskew logic and reducing register count. The current design has:
- Input skew: 0+1+2+3+4+5+6+7 = 28 8-bit registers
- PE array: 64 PEs, each with w(8b) + a_east(8b) + psum_sum(19b) + psum_carry(19b) = 54 bits/PE = 3456 bits
- Output deskew: 0+1+2+3+4+5+6+7 = 28 19-bit registers = 532 bits
- Valid pipe: 15 bits
- Total: ~4000 bits

The PE array dominates. Can I reduce the PE register count? The a_east register is needed for the horizontal activation flow. The w register is needed for the weight. The psum_sum and psum_carry are needed for the vertical accumulation.

One idea: **merge the a_east register with the multiply input**. Instead of registering a_west to a_east, use a_west directly for the multiply and register it. But a_east is the input to the next PE, so it needs to be registered for timing.

Let me try a different approach: go back to the carry-propagate adder (non-CSA) but with a narrower accumulator. The CSA approach trades area (2× registers) for delay. Let me try the non-CSA 19-bit design with a higher target_delay to see if area savings offset the delay increase.

i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_sum_v[0][j]   = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign psum_carry_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]          = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_sum_v[N][j] + psum_carry_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; Right, removing the a_east register breaks the systolic timing - the activation needs to be registered to maintain the 1-cycle-per-hop delay. The combinational passthrough means activations reach all PEs in the same cycle, breaking the skewing. Let me abandon this.

Let me try a different approach: **reduce the de-skew area** by doing the final carry-propagate add after the de-skew, so the de-skew carries the raw sum and carry (2×19=38 bits) instead of the 19-bit result. Wait, that's more bits, not less. 

Actually, what if I move the final CPA into the de-skew pipeline? The CPA (sum + carry) is combinational and currently sits at the array output. If I do the CPA after the de-skew, the de-skew needs to carry both sum and carry (38 bits per column). That's more area. Let me stick with the current approach.

Wait, this is identical to design_csa19.sv. Let me think more creatively. Let me try a different approach to the multiplier: instead of a single 8×8 multiply, use the unsigned multiply with sign correction added directly into the CSA. This way the sign correction terms can be absorbed into the CSA add rather than being part of the multiplier itself.
  -> create_file(content="// TPU - CSA 19-bit with unsigned multiply + sign correction in CSA\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_sum_north,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_carry_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_sum_south,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_carry_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   // Unsigned multiply: a_u * w_u (16-bit unsigned)\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod_u = a_west * w;\n   \n   // Sign correction for signed multiply:\n   // signed_a * signed_w = unsigned(a) * unsigned(w) \n   //   - (a[7] ? w_ext : 0) * 2^DW\n   //   - (w[7] ? a_ext : 0) * 2^DW\n   //   + (a[7] & w[7]) * 2^(2*DW)\n   // \n   // The correction is: subtract a[7]*w*256, subtract w[7]*a*256, add a[7]*w[7]*65536\n   // In 2's complement: subtracting X = adding (~X + 1)\n   // \n   // We can build the signed product by taking prod_u and adding the correction.\n   // The correction terms:\n   //   corr = -a[7]*{8'b0, w} - w[7]*{8'b0, a} + a[7]*w[7]*{16'b1}\n   // This is a 17-bit value.\n   \n   wire [DW-1:0] w_neg = a_west[DW-1] ? w : {DW{1'b0}};   // w if a<0\n   wire [DW-1:0] a_neg = w[DW-1] ? a_west : {DW{1'b0}};   // a if w<0\n   wire sign_both = a_west[DW-1] & w[DW-1];\n   \n   // Full correction (17-bit):\n   // corr = -{8'h00, w_neg} - {8'h00, a_neg} + {16{sign_both}, sign_both}\n   // = {9'b0, ~w_neg} + 1 + {9'b0, ~a_neg} + 1 + {16{sign_both}, sign_both}\n   // We need a 17-bit signed correction value.\n   wire [16:0] corr = ~{9'b0, w_neg} + ~{9'b0, a_neg} + 17'd2 + {{16{sign_both}}, sign_both};\n   \n   // Final product = prod_u + corr (17-bit)\n   wire [16:0] prod_signed = {1'b0, prod_u} + corr;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-17){prod_signed[16]}}, prod_signed};\n\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_sum   = psum_sum_north ^ psum_carry_north ^ prod_ext;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_carry = ((psum_sum_north & psum_carry_north) |\n                                (psum_sum_north & prod_ext) |\n                                (psum_carry_north & prod_ext)) << 1;\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w              <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east         <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_sum_south   <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         psum_carry_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east           <= a_west;\n            psum_sum_south   <= csa_sum;\n            psum_carry_south <= csa_carry;\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]     a_h        [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0]   psum_sum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0]   psum_carry_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]     w_v        [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_sum_v[0][j]   = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign psum_carry_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]          = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_sum_v[N][j] + psum_carry_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_sum_north   (psum_sum_v[i][j]),\n               .psum_carry_north (psum_carry_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_sum_south   (psum_sum_v[i+1][j]),\n               .psum_carry_south (psum_carry_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam ACCW_INT = 2*DW + $clog2(N);\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   wire [N*ACCW_INT-1:0] c_bot;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW_INT)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));\n\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew\n         localparam D = N - 1 - j;\n         wire [ACCW_INT-1:0] cin = c_bot[j*ACCW_INT +: ACCW_INT];\n         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = {{(ACCW-ACCW_INT){cin[ACCW_INT-1]}}, cin};\n         end\n         else begin : g_deskewn\n            reg [ACCW_INT-1:0] dr [0:D-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= {ACCW_INT{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  dr[0] <= cin;\n                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            wire [ACCW_INT-1:0] dout = dr[D-1];\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = {{(ACCW-ACCW_INT){dout[ACCW_INT-1]}}, dout};\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design_csa19u.sv')

[ASSISTANT]
The sign correction is wrong. The issue is that the unsigned multiply `a_west * w` in Verilog, when both operands are unsigned, treats them as unsigned. But the correction terms need careful handling. Let me think more carefully...

For signed multiply: `signed_a * signed_w = unsigned(a) * unsigned(w) - (a[7] ? 256*w : 0) - (w[7] ? 256*a : 0) + (a[7] & w[7] ? 65536 : 0)`

The `256*w` when w is negative means `256 * unsigned(w)`. And the final product should be the 2's complement signed result. The correction is in the context of 17-bit arithmetic. Let me just verify my correction formula is right by working through an example.

a = -1 (8'hFF = 255 unsigned), w = -1 (8'hFF = 255 unsigned)
Expected: (-1)*(-1) = 1
prod_u = 255*255 = 65025 = 16'hFE01
corr = -255*256 - 255*256 + 65536 = -65280 - 65280 + 65536 = -65024
prod_signed = 65025 + (-65024) = 1. Correct!

So the formula is right. The issue must be in the Verilog implementation. Let me check: `~{9'b0, w_neg}` where w_neg = w (8 bits). This gives `~{9'b0, w}` = `{9'h1FF, ~w}` = 17-bit value. Then adding `17'd2`... Actually the issue is that `~{9'b0, w_neg} + ~{9'b0, a_neg} + 17'd2 + {{16{sign_both}}, sign_both}` involves multiple adds that may overflow. Let me simplify this.

Actually, the correction is: `corr = -{8'b0, w, 8'b0} - {8'b0, a, 8'b0} + {16{sign_both}, sign_both, 16'b0}`... wait, let me be more careful.

The correction value is: `-a[7] * w * 2^8 - w[7] * a * 2^8 + a[7]*w[7]*2^16`

In terms of bit patterns (17-bit signed):
- `-a[7] * w * 256` = if a[7]: `-{1'b0, w, 8'b0}` = `{~{1'b0,w,8'b0} + 1}` (2's complement of {w,8'b0})
- Similarly for the other terms

This is getting complicated. Let me just go back to the working CSA19 design and try other optimizations instead.

[ASSISTANT]
Good, the CSA19 is confirmed at 2.90M. Let me now try a different approach to reduce delay. Looking at the timing path: the multiplier portion (w_reg Q → through multiplier gates → product) takes about 280ps (from 91 to ~373). Then the CSA XOR/MAJ chain takes about 295ps (from 373 to 668).

The CSA path is surprisingly long. The XOR chain for the sum bits goes through multiple XOR/XNOR gates. This is because the CSA computes bit-by-bit, and for the upper bits (16-18), the sign extension creates XOR chains. 

Let me try to optimize the CSA by only computing the product bits that matter and handling sign extension differently. The product is 16 bits (0-15). The accumulator needs 19 bits. For bits 16-18, the product is sign-extended. In the CSA, the sum for bits 16-18 is just XOR of sign bits, which should be simple. But the carry chain from bit 15 propagates up.

Actually, let me try a different CSA implementation using explicit full adders instead of the boolean expressions:
  -> create_file(content="// TPU - CSA 19-bit with explicit full-adder structure\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_sum_north,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_carry_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_sum_south,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_carry_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire signed [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n\n   // Explicit full adder CSA\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_sum;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_carry;\n   \n   genvar b;\n   generate\n      for (b = 0; b < ACCW; b = b + 1) begin : fa\n         // Full adder: sum = a ^ b ^ c, carry = maj(a,b,c)\n         assign csa_sum[b] = psum_sum_north[b] ^ psum_carry_north[b] ^ prod_ext[b];\n         if (b < ACCW-1)\n           assign csa_carry[b+1] = (psum_sum_north[b] & psum_carry_north[b]) |\n                                   (psum_sum_north[b] & prod_ext[b]) |\n                                   (psum_carry_north[b] & prod_ext[b]);\n         else\n           assign csa_carry[b] = 1'b0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_sum_v[0][j]   = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign psum_carry_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]          = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_sum_v[N][j] + psum_carry_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; [ASSISTANT]
The issue is that the explicit full-adder with carry_full[ACCW] being dropped loses the top carry bit. The original CSA uses `<< 1` which properly drops the top bit. The problem is that with carry_full[ACCW:1], bit ACCW-1 of csa_carry is carry_full[ACCW], which is the carry out of the MSB. In the original design, `carry << 1` drops bit ACCW-1 and shifts everything up. Let me trace through:

Original: `csa_carry = (... ) << 1` - this is a 19-bit value where bit 0 is 0, bit k = carry of bit k-1. The carry of bit 18 (MSB) is lost.

My version: `csa_carry_full[ACCW:1]` - bit 0 of csa_carry = carry_full[1] = carry of bit 0. Bit 18 = carry_full[19] = carry of bit 18. Wait, that's 20 bits (indices 1-19), but csa_carry is only 19 bits (0-18). So csa_carry[18] = carry_full[19] = carry of bit 18. This includes the top carry bit, which the original drops!

The original `<< 1` on a 19-bit value: bit 18 of the result = bit 17 of the original (carry of bit 17). The carry of bit 18 is lost. But in my version, bit 18 = carry of bit 18. That's the difference!

Let me fix by setting the top carry bit to 0:
  -> edit_file(filename='design_csa19fa.sv', new_string="   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_carry = {csa_carry_full[ACCW-1:1], 1'b0};", old_string='   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_carry = csa_carry_full[ACCW:1];')

  -> replace_file(content="// TPU - CSA 19-bit with explicit full-adder structure (BEST)\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_sum_north,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_carry_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_sum_south,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_carry_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire signed [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_sum;\n   wire [ACCW:0] csa_carry_full;\n   assign csa_carry_full[0] = 1'b0;\n   \n   genvar b;\n   generate\n      for (b = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_sum_north   (psum_sum_v[i][j]),\n               .psum_carry_north (psum_carry_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_sum_south   (psum_sum_v[i+1][j]),\n               .psum_carry_south (psum_carry_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam ACCW_INT = 2*DW + $clog2(N);\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; [ASSISTANT]
Target delay 500 is still the best. Let me now try a different approach: use a **hybrid CSA/carry-propagate** where the lower bits use carry-propagate (since the multiplier produces them) and only the upper bits use carry-save. 

Actually, let me try a more targeted optimization. Looking at the timing path, the critical path goes through the multiplier (NOR/XOR/XNOR chain ~280ps) then the CSA (MAJ/A2O1O1I/O2A1O1I chain ~370ps). The CSA carry path is surprisingly long because the MAJ gates chain together through the carry propagation.

Wait - in a CSA, there IS no carry propagation between bits! Each bit is independent. The issue is that the carry output of bit b feeds into bit b+1 of the next PE's CSA. So within one PE, the CSA is all parallel. The delay I see must be from the multiplier's internal carry chain plus the CSA's MAJ gates.

Let me look more carefully at the path: it starts from `g_skew[3].g_skewn.sr[2]` (the skew register for row 3), goes through `u_array.g_pe_row[3].g_pe_col[0].u_pe` to `psum_sum_south`. So the path is: skew_reg → a_west → multiplier → CSA → psum_sum_south_reg. The multiplier is the big part.

Let me try using a **precomputed weight negation** approach. Since the weight is stationary, I could precompute partial products when the weight is loaded and store them. But that would increase the weight storage significantly.

Let me try a completely different approach: **reduce the multiplier to 7x7 by handling the sign bit separately**. The 8th bit of each operand is the sign bit. If I multiply the 7-bit magnitudes and handle signs separately, the core multiplier is smaller (7×7 vs 8×8).

Actually, let me try something simpler first: use `a * w` without `$signed()` and handle the sign manually. Or try `{{DW{1'b0}}, a_west} * {{DW{1'b0}}, w}` with sign extension. Let me also try a different coding of the multiply.
  -> create_file(content="// TPU - CSA 19-bit, try different multiplier coding\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_sum_north,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_carry_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_sum_south,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_carry_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   // Try: use signed extension before multiply\n   wire signed [DW:0] a_ext = $signed(a_west);\n   wire signed [DW:0] w_ext = $signed(w);\n   wire signed [2*DW+1:0] prod = a_ext * w_ext;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW-2){prod[2*DW+1]}}, prod};\n\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_sum;\n   wire [ACCW:0] csa_carry_full;\n   assign csa_carry_full[0] = 1'b0;\n   \n   genvar b;\n   generate\n      for (b = 0; b < ACCW; b = b + 1) begin : fa\n         assign csa_sum[b] = psum_sum_north[b] ^ psum_carry_north[b] ^ prod_ext[b];\n         assign csa_carry_full[b+1] = (psum_sum_north[b] & psum_carry_north[b]) |\n                                      (psum_sum_north[b] & prod_ext[b]) |\n                                      (psum_carry_north[b] & prod_ext[b]);\n      end\n   endgenerate\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_carry = {csa_carry_full[ACCW-1:1], 1'b0};\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w              <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east         <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_sum_south   <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         psum_carry_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east           <= a_west;\n            psum_sum_south   <= csa_sum;\n            psum_carry_south <= csa_carry;\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]     a_h        [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0]   psum_sum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0]   psum_carry_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]     w_v        [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_sum_v[0][j]   = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign psum_carry_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]          = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_sum_v[N][j] + psum_carry_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_sum_north   (psum_sum_v[i][j]),\n               .psum_carry_north (psum_carry_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_sum_south   (psum_sum_v[i+1][j]),\n               .psum_carry_south (psum_carry_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam ACCW_INT = 2*DW + $clog2(N);\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; [ASSISTANT]
Same result - Yosys optimizes to the same netlist regardless of the coding style. Let me try a fundamentally different approach to reduce area: **share the de-skew logic with the final CPA**. 

Actually, let me try to reduce the number of CSA bits. The product is 16 bits (0-15). The accumulator needs bits 0-18. For bits 16-18, the product is just sign extension. So the CSA for bits 16-18 is just XOR of the sign bit with the sum and carry, and the carry is just AND of the sign bit with sum/carry. This is simpler. But Yosys already optimizes this.

Let me try a different idea: **use a 4:2 compressor** instead of 3:2 CSA. A 4:2 compressor takes 4 inputs and produces sum + carry in one level. But I only have 3 inputs. I could add a 4th input of 0, which would make it equivalent to a 3:2 CSA.

Let me try reducing the accumulator to 18 bits. The maximum value: 8 × 127 × 127 = 129032. In signed: max positive = 129032, max negative = -130048 (8 × -128 × 127). -130048 needs 18 bits signed (2^17 = 131072 > 130048). So 18 bits should work!
  -> create_file(content="// TPU - CSA 18-bit accumulator\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 18)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_sum_north,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_carry_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_sum_south,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_carry_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire signed [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_sum;\n   wire [ACCW:0] csa_carry_full;\n   assign csa_carry_full[0] = 1'b0;\n   \n   genvar b;\n   generate\n      for (b = 0; b < ACCW; b = b + 1) begin : fa\n         assign csa_sum[b] = psum_sum_north[b] ^ psum_carry_north[b] ^ prod_ext[b];\n         assign csa_carry_full[b+1] = (psum_sum_north[b] & psum_carry_north[b]) |\n                                      (psum_sum_north[b] & prod_ext[b]) |\n                                      (psum_carry_north[b] & prod_ext[b]);\n      end\n   endgenerate\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_carry = {csa_carry_full[ACCW-1:1], 1'b0};\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w              <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east         <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_sum_south   <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         psum_carry_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east           <= a_west;\n            psum_sum_south   <= csa_sum;\n            psum_carry_south <= csa_carry;\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 18)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]     a_h        [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0]   psum_sum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0]   psum_carry_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]     w_v        [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0;
